Detailed route:

A. Define labeled moduli spaces:

a) Let 
M_{0,B} = { configurations of 5 distinct labeled points (z_0,z_1,z_2,z_3,w) in 
P^1 } / PSL_2(C).
Let M_{0,A} similarly for 4 points. The forgetful map 
π: M_{0,B} -> M_{0,A}
 is a holomorphic fiber bundle with fiber 
P^1 
minus the four A-points.
Teich(B) is the universal orbifold cover of M_{0,B}; since sphere with marked points has trivial automorphisms for n≥3 generically but orbifold points? For labeled distinct points, automorphism group trivial for n≥3? For n=4, configurations with cross-ratio special might have automorphisms? But Teich as universal cover of configuration quotient; mapping class group acts. We can ignore orbifold by using fine moduli of labeled points with level structure? For n≥5, generic automorphism trivial; but some points have automorphisms? Labeled points on P^1: a Möbius transformation fixing three labeled points is identity, so for n≥3 no nontrivial automorphism preserving labels. Thus M_{0,n} is a genuine manifold (configuration quotient) for labeled points. Good. Teich(S^2,B) is the universal cover of M_{0,B}; mapping class group (pure braid group of sphere) is deck group. For n=4, labeled automorphism also trivial. Good.

b) Let 
R ⊂ M_{0,B}
 be the locus of labeled configurations (a0,a1,a2,a3,v) for which there exists a quadratic rational map R with a0 critical, R(a_i)=a_{i+1}, and R(c2)=v, v not in A. We can use algebraic normal form to show R is the graph of a holomorphic map V over a Zariski open subset S⊂M_{0,A}. Specifically, in coordinates (a0,a1,a2,a3)=(0,∞,1,λ), V(λ) formula; S = P^1 minus {0,1,∞,1/2, roots ...}. The projection π:R->S is biholomorphic. This proves moduli image.

B. Relate D_f to preimage/lifts of R.

Given topological f with sets A,B, D_f = {τ∈Teich(B): f^*τ=i_*τ}. There is a standard theorem (Thurston's characterization, or McMullen) that the projection 
q_B: Teich(B) -> M_{0,B}

The definition D_f uses fixed f; but the problem statement says its image in moduli is set of conjugacy classes. It does not say D_f is the full preimage. Final theorem statement:

```
Theorem. Let a_i=f^i(c_1) and v=f(c_2). Let E={a_0,a_2}, O={a_1,a_3,v}. Let γ be any simple closed curve separating E from O whose lift under f has a component δ separating {a_1,a_3} from {a_0,a_2,c_2}. Then T_γ(D_f)=D_f. Moreover the induced permutation of π_0(D_f) has infinite orbits; in fact if C is any component and n≠0, T_γ^n(C)≠C.
```
